So, I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: When your mind wanders off to things that could go wrong, do you experience physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shaking?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't really noticed anything like that. My heart seems fine, and I don't... I don't get shaky or anything. It's mostly just the distraction, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I still enjoy things, like the painting. It's just... hard to focus on them sometimes, like I said.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fatigued or have you noticed a significant loss of energy lately, even when you haven't been physically active?\n\nPatient: ...I suppose so. By midafternoon, I just... I feel pretty worn out. It's the sort of tired that a nap or a quiet morning doesn't really fix. Just... simple things, you know? Like weeding the garden, or... or standing at the easel. They just feel a bit heavier than they used to. But... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately?\n\nPatient: No... not that I've noticed. I still eat about the same, I suppose. And my weight hasn't really changed. Just... normal, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or felt excessive guilt about things lately?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I haven't really... done anything to feel guilty about. I just do my work at the practice and... well, that's about it. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or in a low mood most of the day, nearly every day, even if you wouldn't describe it as depression?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I worry a lot about things, but... I wouldn't say I feel down. Just... on edge, I suppose. Not sad.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, sleep has been... a bit difficult, I suppose. I get into bed tired enough, but... once it's quiet, my mind just starts. I end up replaying things from the practice—patient transfers, the financials, that sort of thing. It just keeps going. And even if I do manage to drift off, I'm usually awake again by four or five. It just... doesn't feel like I really rested.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you’ve lost the ability to feel pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, I still enjoy things well enough, I suppose. It's not that I feel low. Like I mentioned before, it's... it's just more about being tense. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're having trouble making decisions or that your thoughts feel slowed down lately?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I make decisions at the practice just fine. And my thoughts aren't slow. Like I said, it's... it's more that they just don't stop. But no, they aren't slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period like this before, where you felt tired and had trouble sleeping, or has this been a new experience for you?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I've always been a bit of a worrier, I suppose. But it hasn't been like this. So, no, this is... new.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I've never really been a depressed sort of person. Just... just the worrying, really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
